## Consequential corrections

- Scan 077, Path chain: heads on first and last connecting shafts: <code>x_0=x\to x_1\to x_2\,\mathord{-}\,\mathord{-}\to x_n=y</code>. Only arrow interpretation was checked against visible terminal strokes. No proof was checked.
- Scan 077, Inserted f_l(u): <code>\inserted{$f_l(u)$,}</code>. The inserted f-family is visible and must not be dropped; no sufficiency statement was proved.
- Scan 077, Deleted subscript inside g: <code>g_{\deleted{\unsure{yx}}l}(u)</code>. The deletion state and attachment are secure; the cancelled index letters remain uncertain.
- Scan 077, Entire oblique margin note: <code>\margin{On a deux façons de ``récupérer&#x27;&#x27; l&#x27;opération de $\Gamma$ sur le groupoïde $\widehat{\Pi}_1(X,I)$ (où $I\subset X_K$) en quelque sorte duales l&#x27;une de l&#x27;autre: soit on prend un syst. de $l_j:x_j\mathbin{-}y_j$ $(x_j,y_j\in I)$ générateurs pour $\Pi_1(X,I)$, et regarde les $f_{l_j}(u)$ ou $g_{l_j}(u)$, soit on fixe des $T_\alpha$ comme dessus, et on regarde les $g^\alpha_{x,y}$ $(\alpha\in A,x,y\in T_\alpha\cap I)$. En fait on voit très bientôt qu&#x27;il est \deleted{\unsure{ff}} inutile de supposer que les $T_\alpha$ recouvrent $X$ (p.ex. si les $T_\alpha$ sont les supports des chemins $l_j$).}</code>. The note is transcribed, not accepted as a verified proof of reconstruction. Its g-indices are x,y, whereas those in the body are y,x; both orders are retained.
- Scan 077, X in final paragraph: <code>on a maintenu $X$, $u$ fixes</code>. Both X and u are held fixed in this sentence. This is a restoration of visible text, not a mathematical repair.
- Scan 078, Qualification to (28), groupoid sign: <code>\unsure{\widehat\Pi_1(X)&#124;\Pi_1(X)}</code>. The next prose path condition is unhatted Π₁(X); no completion convention was inferred.
- Scan 078, Reference after d’où on tire par: <code>(\unsure{6&#124;8})</code>. No earlier numbered formula inspected; an ink comparison only.
- Scan 078, Cancelled raised character before g_l in (29): <code>\deleted{\unsure{u}}</code>. A cancelled action u is mathematically plausible, but the surviving ink does not establish it.
- Scan 078, First f in the i.e. sentence: <code>\unsure{f_{l\alpha}&#124;f_l}</code>. Equation (30) and the other occurrences use f_l; that does not erase this mark.
- Scan 078, Isomorphism diagram, arrow and alphabets: <code>\widehat\Pi_1(X,x)\arrow[r,&quot;\Pi_1(l)&quot;]\widehat\Pi_1(X,y)</code>. Path-induced isomorphism from the x-based group to the y-based group. No equivariance added.
- Scan 078, Oblique margin, couple and bicocycle phrase: <code>couple $(f_l,g_l)$ comme un ``bicocycle&#x27;&#x27; sur $\Gamma$, relatif à un $\Gamma$-bitorseur</code>. Pair described relative to a bitorseur; no definition or proof here.
- Scan 078, Oblique margin, Hom object: <code>\operatorname{Hom}_{\widehat\Pi_1(X)}(x,y)</code>. Below Γ-bitorseur appear Hom, a hatted capital Π₁(X) subscript, (x,y), and sous. H drops the Hom object.
- Scan 079, Struck expression beneath g_l(u) in (31): <code>\deleted{\unsure{{}^ua\cdot a^{-1}&#124;a^{-1}\cdot{}^ua}}</code>. The possible term {}^u a·a^{-1} resembles the left-coboundary expression. That context is only supporting evidence; the visible attachment of the final exponent is decisive for the preference. Both candidates remain marked.
- Scan 079, Left-cocycle qualification above the second prose line: <code>\inserted{à g.}</code>. The text distinguishes right and left cocycles. The mathematical sense supports attachment to 1-cocycle, while its vertical position shows insertion.
- Scan 079, Subscript under the first H^1 in (32): <code>H^1_{\unsure{g}}</code>. The dr subscript in the next line is clear. The paired notation supports g for gauche but does not by itself settle whether a stroke is deletion.
- Scan 079, Final prose: plural abbreviated isom phrase: <code>via les isom extérieurs canoniques</code>. Outer isomorphisms relate the completed fundamental groups. No expansion of isom is made in source text.
- Scan 079, Final diagram: isom / ext / tilde labels: <code>\substack{\mathrm{isom}\\\mathrm{ext}\\\sim}</code>. The labels isom then ext then a short tilde are stacked above the single right-pointing arrow shaft. No label lies below it.
- Scan 079, Margin: condition before l:x→y: <code>\unsure{qu&#x27;on exige que}</code>. After sans, a q-descender/apostrophe sequence and short joined words support qu’on exige que. The phrase is interfered with by the main text, so doubt is retained rather than replacing it with H’s supposer.
- Scan 079, Margin: only the completed group is required: <code>seulement de $\widehat\Pi_1(X)$</code>. The rotated note shows seulement followed by the short word de and the hatted Π expression. There is no l∈ on this sloping line; nearby main-text letters must not be merged into the note.
- Scan 079, Margin: opening of second sentence: <code>Et la définition des classes de</code>. The sloping sentence continues Et la définition des classes de. The horizontal x,y∈X_K belongs to the main paragraph, at a different angle, not to this sentence.
- Scan 079, Margin: subscript of f in classes de: <code>f_{\unsure{l&#124;y}}</code>. Classes of the cocycles f_l and g_l fit the preceding definitions. The contextual fit only supports the visible l-shaped loop; it does not erase uncertainty.
- Scan 079, Margin: subscript of g in classes de: <code>g_{\unsure{l&#124;y}}</code>. The companion g has the same small loop-foot subscript form as f, best read l. Overlap from the horizontal prose makes the outline less secure than the first marginal f_l(u),g_l(u).
- Scan 079, Margin: parenthesis and start of independence statement: <code>(ou classes de cohomologie) (32) ne dépend pas des</code>. The rotated marginal lines read the whole parenthetical ou classes de cohomologie, a small sloping (32), then ne dépend pas des. These words are distinct from the larger upright display number.

- Scan 082, Reduction verb near foot: <code>est réduit</code>. The main line starts est, then réduit with the short initial r and accented ending; H’s revient does not fit. The diagonal note lies above-left and is distinct.

- Scan 094, Theta with connected upper stroke before possibles: <code>\unsure{\widetilde{\Theta}&#124;\Theta}</code>. The prose parametrizes lifted maps, which is compatible with a tilde, but does not decide the connected ink. The two readings remain explicit.

Root separately checked the right-action claim: right Pi0-prime suffices, and the normalizer is the full right multiplier group when the transporter is nonempty; unrestricted Pi0 is false in general. This reader claims no separate proof event.

### Remaining doubts

- small mark after I_beta: <code>\unsure{et&#124;,}</code>; A dark epsilon-shaped hooked mark is present. It differs from the adjacent ordinary comma, but may be an abbreviated connective or idiosyncratic punctuation. (low).
- deleted subscript before surviving l of g: <code>\deleted{\unsure{yx}}</code>; Dense cancellation hides the central strokes. The y-like descender favours y first, but is crossed by the strike. (low).
- deleted fragment before inutile in the margin: <code>\deleted{\unsure{ff}}</code>; Two tall crossed strokes survive, but the strike coincides with the crossbars; the cancelled letters cannot be identified securely. (low).

### Remaining doubts

- reference after par: <code>(\unsure{6&#124;8})</code>; Tall returning top stroke resembles the other 6 forms, but narrow upper loop admits 8. Root independently favours 6; earlier numbered reference not checked. (medium).
- cancelled prefix in (29): <code>\deleted{\unsure{u}}</code>; Dense cancellation leaves no decisive letter anatomy; plausible action u is background only. (low).
- first f in i.e. sentence: <code>\unsure{f_{l\alpha}&#124;f_l}</code>; Extra loop is physically present; exact letter and function unresolved. (low).
- after induit par l: <code>\unsure{\ldots\ \deleted{$l$}&#124;\deleted{en $l$}}</code>; The existing crop shows three compact dark blobs on one baseline, followed after a white gap by a taller l-like form with a dense overwrite at its top. No common cancellation stroke clearly crosses all three blobs. A live ellipsis followed by a canceled l-like sign is favored; connected low letters en under cancellation remain possible, so the uncertainty applies to the entire fragment rather than only the word inside a definite deletion. (low).
- curve at multiplicativité: <code>\inserted{exprim.} \unsure{``multiplicativité en $l$&#x27;&#x27;}</code>; Long curve compatible with insertion positioning; cannot itself prove a deletion. (medium).
- inserted un 1-cocycl...: <code>\unsure{cocycles&#124;cocycle}</code>; Final e/es inseparable; grammar unused. (low).
- margin after expliciter: <code>\unsure{au demeurant&#124;un de vue qui voit} le</code>; Competing segmentations in linked oblique writing; root observation prompted promotion of au demeurant, uncertainty retained. (medium).
- Hat at first display’s Π₁(X): <code>\unsure{\widehat\Pi_1(X)&#124;\Pi_1(X)}</code>; Caret-like pair above Π overlaps descending y; the final focused crop prevents calling it wholly separate. (medium).

### Remaining doubts

- Deleted les: <code>\deleted{\unsure{les&#124;le}}</code>; Remainder under the heavy horizontal cancellation is not fully exposed. (medium).
- Deleted underbrace in (31): <code>\deleted{\unsure{{}^ua\cdot a^{-1}&#124;a^{-1}\cdot{}^ua}}</code>; Final exponent attachment is visible; left superscript lies under diagonal hatching. (low).
- M0315: <code>\unsure{enfin}</code>; Sloping marginal writing crosses the baseline word. (medium).
- M0317: <code>\unsure{g}</code>; Loop and descender survive; the blob may be retracing. No separate deletion stroke is seen. (medium).
- Deleted donc: <code>\deleted{\unsure{donc}}</code>; The single horizontal strike masks the upper letter forms. (low).
- M0318: <code>\unsure{déjà de type&#124;de type}</code>; Compact extra group before de type remains difficult despite rotation. (low).
- M0319: <code>\unsure{qu&#x27;on exige que}</code>; Main-text strokes interfere with parts of the sloping phrase. (medium).

- M0322: <code>\unsure{fixation du}</code>; The middle strokes are compressed and partially intersect other ink. (medium).
- Slanted margin, path l:x to y after the phrase sans qu’on exige que: <code>$l:x\unsure{\longrightarrow&#124;\mathbin{-}}y$</code>; The shaft is visible, with a short near-vertical dark termination. I cannot separate an oblique barb at this small marginal occurrence. The main-text path has a genuine compact head, so arrow is retained first, but the margin alone does not settle arrow versus undirected stroke. (medium).

### Remaining doubts

- M0323 overwritten f subscript: <code>f_{\unsure{y,x&#124;x,y}}</code>; Overwriting leaves competing index traces. The preferred y,x fits the clear adjacent g_{y,x}, but that is not sufficient to erase palaeographic doubt. (medium).
- Deleted un: <code>\deleted{\unsure{un}}</code>; Black cancellation masks most letter structure even in the crop. (low).
- Deleted vient: <code>\deleted{\unsure{vient}}</code>; Only the i dot and terminal traces stand apart from the deletion line. (low).
- M0326 live final verb: <code>\unsure{pour&#124;joue}</code>; The p/j and final r/e shapes remain ambiguous; preserve C first reading rather than normalizing to the grammatical expectation. (medium).

### Remaining doubts

- overwritten target superscript: <code>\widehat\Pi^{\unsure{\text{ét}}}(x_0)</code>; Both component letters are heavily retraced; the lower curved stroke may be an older layer. The visible ink is not omitted. (low).
- cancelled old g index: <code>\deleted{\unsure{x_0}}</code>; Diagonal cancellation hides part of the x and small lower rounded sign. Cancellation and attachment to the subscript region are certain. (medium).
- isolated initial glyph: <code>\unsure{4&#124;\P}</code>; Closed triangular shape and single descending stem fit a handwritten 4 but do not exclude a cursive paragraph sign. There is no contextual numbering on this page that decides it. (low).
- cancelled wording after Comme: <code>\deleted{\unsure{on} \unsure{un}}</code>; Two clusters remain partly visible. Their preferred readings on and un remain uncertain; no separate a is securely visible. (low).
- placement state of 1-cocycle: <code>\unsure{\inserted{$1$-cocycle}}</code>; Raised and smaller writing favours an insertion; no caret settles the state. Doubt is marked in the text. (medium).

### Remaining doubts

- cancelled en donne: <code>\deleted{\unsure{en donne}}</code>; Strike obscures lower connected letters; initial e/n and d favour C but do not settle it. (low).
- cancelled un after correspondant à: <code>\deleted{\unsure{un}}</code>; Small upright and connected remainder obscured by heavy strike. (low).
- margin phrase after particulière: <code>qui \unsure{bien plus intéressante&#124;des plus intéressantes}</code>; Only plus and intéressant- are partly recoverable; first word and ending unresolved. (low).
- first unreadable margin interval: <code>\illegible{?}</code>; Overlapping oblique and main-line writing prevents recovery or a reliable word count. (low).
- problem/formula in margin: <code>\unsure{un problème&#124;une formule}</code>; Tall looped letter and short preceding word permit competing segmentations. (low).
- variable/valable in margin: <code>\unsure{variable&#124;valable}</code>; Internal linked letters obscured. (low).
- margin en: <code>\unsure{en}</code>; Two short joined marks remain uncertain. (low).
- margin au-dessus de: <code>\unsure{au-dessus de}</code>; C’s words not securely separable; retained only as uncertain reading. (low).
- margin Q-like sign: <code>\unsure{\mathbb Q}</code>; A rounded crossed form is visible but not sufficient to certify Q or its typeface. (low).
- terminal margin interval and punctuation: <code>\illegible{?}\unsure{.)}</code>; Overlapping oblique and main-line writing prevents recovery or a reliable word count. The final point and closing-parenthesis boundary also remain uncertain. (low).
- Live insertion above the cancelled en donne: <code>\inserted{\unsure{connait&#124;connaît}}</code>; The i-position has a detached compact dark mark. In the saved enlargement it looks point-like, without separately distinguishable arms of a circumflex. The letters connai-t and the insertion state are otherwise supported. Plain i is preferred visually, but a compressed circumflex cannot be excluded. (medium).

### Remaining doubts

- Display (35), alphabet of U: <code>\unsure{\mathbb U&#124;U}_{0,3}</code>; Doubled left stem favors blackboard form, but may be retracing; bold form in C is also plausible as a typographic interpretation. (low).
- considérant, overworked d-region: <code>\unsure{considérant}</code>; The lower loop is real ink within the word, but its correction/deletion status cannot be settled. It is not evidence of a separate canceled word before the word. (medium).
- Display (36), joining stroke: <code>\unsure{\mathbin{-}&#124;\to}</code>; The terminal downward nub is too blunt/short to distinguish a pen ending from a minimally formed barb securely. (low).

### Remaining doubts

- Second paragraph, first connaît: est connue quand on connaît son opération: <code>\unsure{connait&#124;connaît}</code>; The mark above i is one short thick oblique stroke, slightly curved, detached from the low i-to-t join. No separate rising and falling sides of a circumflex can be distinguished. A dot-like stroke is favored; a compact circumflex remains possible. (medium).
- Second paragraph, second connaît: et si de plus on connaît son opération: <code>\unsure{connait&#124;connaît}</code>; The i mark is a short detached oblique oval or stroke. It has no separable apex or two-sided circumflex form. The visible point favors plain i, with the compressed-accent alternative retained. (medium).

### Remaining doubts

- Margin, exprimer: <code>\unsure{exprimer}</code>; Crossing body strokes crowd the medial letters despite a plausible e/x beginning and -imer ending. (medium).
- Margin, long word after exprimer: <code>\unsure{linéairement&#124;littéralement}</code>; The initial li and final -ment are more secure than the compressed intervening letters. (low).
- Margin, small resp-like abbreviation: <code>\unsure{resp.&#124;resp}</code>; Letter formation and following punctuation remain partly uncertain. (medium).
- Margin, final reference: <code>\unsure{décrit p.8!&#124;dit p.8&#x27;}</code>; Crowded initial letters and the final upright punctuation cannot be fully resolved; p.8 is secure. (low).
- Article before canceled origine: <code>\unsure{d&#x27;un&#124;d&#x27;une}</code>; A separate terminal e is not secure. Root favored un from the terminal stroke; the reader retains une as a live ink possibility. (low).
- Canceled word before et tout particulièrement: <code>\deleted{\unsure{et&#124;est}}</code>; The heavy strike obscures the interval between the e-like stroke and the t. (low).
- symétrie(s): <code>\unsure{symétrie&#124;symétries}</code>; The final curl does not securely distinguish final e from a compressed e-s ending. (medium).
- First connai-t occurrence, before son action: <code>\unsure{connait&#124;connaît}</code>; The detached mark above the final i-region is a compact oblique dark blob. It lacks a distinguishable two-sided crest. A dot-like mark on plain i is favored, but compression of a small circumflex cannot be excluded. (medium).
- Second connai-t occurrence, before les: <code>\unsure{connaît&#124;connait}</code>; The detached mark is longer than at the first occurrence. Its small rounded/rising left start leads into a substantially longer descending right stroke. This gives more support to a circumflex than the first blob, but the two-arm construction remains insufficiently secure to exclude a drawn-out dot or tick. (medium).

### Remaining doubts

- M0367: diagram C: <code>\unsure{\mathcal C&#124;C}</code>; Ornament and retracing do not securely distinguish the alphabet. (medium).
- Cancelled verb after on: <code>\deleted{\unsure{trouve&#124;trouver}}</code>; The terminal letter is hidden by the strike. (low).
- Cancelled word after K est: <code>\deleted{\unsure{de}}</code>; The preceding Gamma descender and cancellation overlap the letters; this lowers C’s certainty without changing its first reading. (low).

- First word of cancelled closing baseline: <code>\unsure{La}</code>; Looped initial and rounded ending survive, but the strike obscures letter and case details. (low).
- First word of upper insertion in cancelled closing clause: <code>\unsure{l&#x27;action&#124;l&#x27;opération}</code>; The middle of the word is crowded and crossed; action is better supported but not secure. (medium).
- First connaît, in est connue quand on connaît l’opération: <code>\unsure{connait&#124;connaît}</code>; The detached i mark is one short thick oblique stroke. It favors a dot-like point but could compress an accent; no distinct pair of circumflex arms can be separated. Keep plain i first and the circumflex alternative. (medium).
- Two small points immediately below/right of the final t in the first connaît: <code>\unsure{:}</code>; Two small detached dark points are visible at the right edge of the first connaît, well below its rising word baseline and to the left of the following l’opération. The upper mark is angular/comma-like and the lower one rounded. They lie in the gap above the next line containing Π̂₁(C,Ĩ), roughly 80 pixels above that line’s highest tilde/hat marks; they do not attach to that following formula. Their presence as ink is clear, but their unusually low position leaves punctuation versus incidental pen-points unresolved. The proposed \unsure{:} records a possible colon only. (low).

### Remaining doubts

- First sentence, déterminé(e): <code>\unsure{déterminée&#124;déterminé}</code>; Final strokes do not settle the number of e letters. (low).
- Hom target, first argument: <code>\unsure{m&#124;x}</code>; Ink is m-like; expected x does not settle the missing crossing. (medium).
- First cancelled X: <code>\deleted{$\unsure{X}$}</code>; Hatching obscures the character. (low).
- Cancelled character before I in Π̂_1(C;…I): <code>\deleted{\unsure{x}}</code>; The surviving curled cluster is insufficient to identify x securely. (low).
- Second U formula, cropped right edge: <code>\unsure{)}</code>; Only the lower terminal is visible; H comma cannot be verified. (medium).

### Remaining doubts

- Upper inserted word after et: <code>\unsure{projetant&#124;\deleted{supposant}}</code>; Cramped initial/medial strokes and their state remain ambiguous; projetant is preferred, while canceled supposant is viable. (low).
- Terminal point of surj: <code>\unsure{surj.&#124;surj}</code>; The abbreviated surj sequence is plausible, but its terminal point is not secure at the crowded edge. (medium).
- Marginal word after Regarder des: <code>\unsure{familles}</code>; Crossed f-like start and two tall looped forms support familles, but body overlap obscures the middle. (low).
- Two unreadable groups after marginal familles: <code>\illegible{2}</code>; Two dark compressed word-like groups following the first-line candidate remain physically unreadable in the available crop. They are kept as two unreadable words. (low).
- Marginal word after canceled telles: <code>\unsure{conditions}</code>; Conditions is the best reading of the dark lower-line word after canceled telles, but crossed strokes prevent complete certainty. (medium).
- Words after marginal conditions: <code>\unsure{de} \illegible{1}</code>; The first short portion looks like de; the following dark word cannot be securely read independently of the pale body beneath. (low).
- Canceled phrase beneath que l’autom.: <code>\deleted{\unsure{l&#x27;opération de}}</code>; The earlier canceled phrase is partially hidden by que l’autom.; the C first reading remains plausible. (low).
- Canceled qui soit ponctu…: <code>\deleted{\unsure{qui soit ponctuel&#124;qui soit ponctuellement}}</code>; The crossed line supports qui soit ponctu-, but its ending at the edge remains unsettled. (low).
- Canceled marginal word before conditions: <code>\deleted{\unsure{telles}}</code>; The heavily canceled marginal word is tentatively telles. (low).
- Older letter under current C: <code>\deleted{\unsure{U}}\inserted{C}_{0K}</code>; A U-like earlier outline is distinguishable below the later C. Its identity remains doubtful, although the current C_{0K} is clear. (low).
- Later quantifier before rev.: <code>\unsure{\forall&#124;\mathrm{tt}}</code>; The looped/pointed sign is better read as ∀, but joined tt remains possible. (low).
- Final punctuation after preimage formula: <code>\unsure{---&#124;.}</code>; The last mark is dash-like, but could be a strongly elongated full stop. (medium).

### Remaining doubts

- Opening preposition before continuité: <code>d\unsure{é&#124;e}</code>; The detached rising tick may be an acute accent or a pen mark. (medium).
- Joining sign between the lemma's C' and C: <code>\unsure{\mathbin{-}&#124;\to}</code>; Small terminal spur without an unequivocal reverse barb; a dash is preferred. (medium).
- Mark immediately before de sorte: <code>\unsure{\deleted{et}&#124;(}</code>; Extra internal strokes suggest cancellation, but a retraced parenthesis cannot be excluded. (low).

### Remaining doubts

- First display superscript: <code>\unsure{\prime&#124;*}</code>; Upper mark is short and branched. (medium).
- Upper annotation target superscript: <code>\unsure{*&#124;\prime}</code>; Retracing obscures decoration and any earlier state. (low).
- First cancelled word: <code>\deleted{\unsure{soit}}</code>; Deletion obscures the compressed letters. (low).
- First paragraph right-edge punctuation: <code>\unsure{.}</code>; The last mark meets the black image boundary. (low).
- Cancelled phrase after il: <code>\unsure{y en a un de}</code>; Middle strokes lie under a straight cancellation. (low).
- Point after cancelled phrase: <code>\unsure{.}</code>; The separate point lies beyond the strike’s end. (low).
- Live parenthetical label: <code>(\unsure{abrég.})</code>; Compressed letters; no independent cancellation. (low).
- Cancelled x before xᵢ: <code>\deleted{\unsure{x}}</code>; Possible trailing material cannot be separated from cancellation. (low).
- Cancelled group expression: <code>\deleted{$\unsure{\Pi_1(}$}</code>; Large opening curve is visible, while the group glyph remains crossed. (medium).
- Underbrace first word: <code>\unsure{espace&#124;esp.}</code>; Extra low strokes after esp may spell ace. (low).
- Point after hom: <code>\unsure{hom&#124;hom.}</code>; The letters are clear but no point is secure. (low).
- Point after g: <code>\unsure{g&#124;g.}</code>; Long looped g without a secure independent following point. (low).

### Remaining doubts

- Cancelled note under initial quotient: <code>\unsure{esp&#124;espace}</code>; The first esp-like strokes are visible, but the low right continuation is obscured. (low).
- Upper mark of inserted first Θ de Θ: <code>\unsure{\widetilde\Theta&#124;\Theta&#x27;}</code>; The mark overlaps the descender from hypothèse. (medium).
- Cancelled word after Pi₀-ensemble: <code>\unsure{un}</code>; The thick strike hides the opening letter shape. (low).
- Cancelled word after correspondent: <code>\unsure{aux}</code>; The middle letters are almost entirely under the strike. (low).
- Cancelled word before soit compatible: <code>\unsure{comment&#124;convenant}</code>; Only the initial c and final t are secure. (low).
- Abbreviation in the long margin after un: <code>\unsure{s-grp}</code>; The short abbreviation is clear as an abbreviation; exact grp letters remain crowded. (medium).
- Cancelled superscript in (46): <code>\unsure{0}</code>; A small zero-like loop is obscured by cancellation. (low).
- Upper expression cancelled after puisque: <code>\unsure{x&#x27;\in E_0}</code>; A second, upper expression is real but heavily crossed; the complete formula is tentative. (low).
- Short form before lower live x'∈E₀: <code>\unsure{\mathrm{pr}&#124;\forall}</code>; Pr is only a tentative reading; q/t descenders can produce a misleading quantifier-like shape. (low).
- Second inserted lifted-action label, above dans in the middle paragraph: <code>\unsure{\overline\Theta&#124;\widetilde\Theta}</code>; The detached mark over this inserted Θ is nearly horizontal, with a short downward bend at its left end and no clear full wave. The nearby mark over C in the same crop has a distinct rising and falling wave. A bar is the first candidate; a flattened tilde remains possible. This local doubt does not change the separate earlier tilde/prime doubt. (medium).
- Closing paragraph, connaît after est connue quand on: <code>\unsure{connait&#124;connaît}</code>; The detached mark over i is compact and hooked, with a short curved outline rather than two clearly separated circumflex arms. It favors a dot-like mark, but a compressed accent remains possible. Retain plain i first with the circumflex alternative. (medium).

### Remaining doubts

- Inserted point label: <code>\inserted{$\unsure{x&#x27;_1&#124;x&#x27;&#x27;}$}</code>; Two small right-hand strokes are unusually high; index 1 plus prime and double prime cannot be securely separated. (low).
- Cramped words after en dépend: <code>\unsure{aux de l&#x27;}</code>; An aux-like initial group followed by de l’ is the best literal reading; cramped joins prevent a secure reading. No grammatical reconstruction is supplied. (low).
- Short word after pas: <code>\unsure{un&#124;en}</code>; The short group between pas and endom. can be read un or en. (low).
- Final défini in outer note: <code>\unsure{défini}</code>; A d-like beginning and tall f are visible, but the crowded ending at the right edge is not completely secure. (medium).
- Canceled field subscript after X: <code>\deleted{_{\unsure{\overline{k}&#124;\overline K}}}</code>; The subscript is definitely hatched through; the barred letter beneath the strokes admits k and K. (low).
- Unbarred field label after I subset X: <code>\unsure{k&#124;K}</code>; The unbarred letter has a lower-case-like rounded right branch, but case is not securely determined. (low).
- Canceled field subscript after X-prime: <code>\deleted{_{\unsure{\overline{k}&#124;\overline K}}}</code>; The second subscript is also definitely hatched; its surviving barred letter remains k/K. (low).
- Canceled opening glyph: <code>\deleted{$\unsure{\widetilde{\mathcal C}&#124;\widetilde E}$}</code>; The canceled small glyph has a C/E-like lower shape and a confused upper ornament. (low).
- Canceled marginal block: Si: <code>\unsure{Si}</code>; Two crossed initial strokes and a dot support Si, but remain obscured. (low).
- Canceled marginal block: \widetilde{\mathcal C}: <code>$\unsure{\widetilde{\mathcal C}}$</code>; A wavy upper mark and open C-like lower curve survive the crossing. (low).
- Canceled marginal block: correspond: <code>\unsure{correspond}</code>; The compressed co-/corres- outline and later descender are plausible beneath the strikes. (low).
- Canceled marginal block: unreadable words: <code>\illegible{3}</code>; Three word-like groups are physically obscured by the heavy crossing. (low).
- Canceled marginal block: on a: <code>\unsure{on a}</code>; Two short groups can tentatively be separated as on a. (low).
- Canceled marginal block: \Theta_0': <code>$\unsure{\Theta&#x27;_0}$</code>; The oval crossed symbol and small adjacent marks support Theta-prime-zero but are not secure. (low).
- Canceled marginal block: unreadable words: <code>\illegible{3}</code>; A second run of three word-like groups remains unreadable. (low).
- Canceled marginal block: \Theta: <code>$\unsure{\Theta}$</code>; An oval/crossbar outline survives in the middle of the cancellation. (low).
- Canceled marginal block: unreadable words: <code>\illegible{2}</code>; Two further word-like groups cannot be read through the hatching. (low).
- Canceled marginal block: \widetilde{\Theta}: <code>$\unsure{\widetilde{\Theta}}$</code>; A tilde-like upper stroke and Theta-like oval are partly visible. (low).
- Canceled marginal block: tel que: <code>\unsure{tel que}</code>; The short words before the existential formula are tentatively tel que. (low).
- Canceled marginal block: \exists\gamma\in\Pi_0: <code>\unsure{\exists\gamma\in\Pi_0}</code>; The existential sign and gamma are fairly clear; the membership target remains partly hidden by crossing strokes. (low).
- Canceled marginal block: avec: <code>\unsure{avec}</code>; A short connected word before the formula is tentatively avec. (low).
- Canceled groupoid after cas: <code>\deleted{$\unsure{\mathcal C&#124;\widetilde{\mathcal C}}$}</code>; The open C is secure, but the upper curve may be a tilde or one of the cancellation strokes. (medium).
- Upper marginal joining stroke: <code>\unsure{\mathbin{-}&#124;\to}</code>; The upper marginal joining stroke is short and blunt; a minimal arrowhead is not securely excluded. (low).
- Punctuation beside (48): <code>\unsure{;&#124;:}</code>; Two dark vertically arranged marks are real; the elongated lower mark favors semicolon, with colon still possible. (medium).
- Newly retained crossed fragment: <code>\unsure{pts}</code>; The separate short canceled row has a possible p-t-s outline. It is not securely legible. (low).
- Remainder of preliminary crossed fragment: <code>\illegible{2}</code>; Two further word-like groups in that preliminary crossed row are physically unreadable. (low).
- Join in inserted I-prime to I: <code>I&#x27;\unsure{\mathbin{-}&#124;\to}I</code>; The tiny joining stroke has a downturned terminal but no fully distinct arrowhead. (medium).
- Added word after qu’un: <code>\inserted{\unsure{endom}}</code>; The newly isolated raised word has a flattened en-like start, tall d, and low om-like tail. The curve attaches after qu’un. Its abbreviated ending is not fully secure; no period is supplied. Root agreed with the cautious reading. (medium).
- E0-like symbol before existential line in canceled margin: <code>\unsure{E_0}</code>; A separate E-like form with surviving horizontal turns and a small lower zero stands just left of the existential line. It is distinct from the crossed prose, but partly covered by cancellation; its exact relation to the neighboring words is unsettled. Reinspection followed root’s pointer to this locus. (medium).
- Theta before possibles and (49): <code>\unsure{\widetilde{\Theta}&#124;\Theta}</code>; A broad, slightly waving cap has a free upturned right end; its left end curves down into the left side of the Theta. No incoming stroke from above supports treating it as the preceding display’s g descender. A connected tilde is favored, but an extended or doubled Theta construction remains possible. (medium).
- First display label: possible opening parenthesis: <code>\unsure{(47)&#124;47)}</code>; The digits 4 and 7 and the right closing parenthesis are clear. The only candidate left parenthesis is a low rising-return stroke touching the bottom of the long 4 stem; it can also be the terminal hook of that 4. There is no separately visible full-height opening curve. The crop does not safely distinguish those readings. (medium).

### Remaining doubts

- Opening relative word: <code>\unsure{qui&#124;que}</code>; Compressed terminal letter. (low).
- Cancelled Pi after equality: <code>\deleted{\unsure{\Pi_1}}</code>; Overcrossing obscures the small subscript. (low).
- Upper cancelled wording: <code>\deleted{\unsure{il considère}}</code>; Cancellation crosses the final letters. (low).
- Lower cancelled prefix: <code>\unsure{il y a}</code>; Distinct low traces exist but overlap the raised phrase. (low).
- Star-map target first argument: <code>\unsure{Y&#124;X}</code>; Current fork is overinked. (low).
- Stable-sous Theta upper mark: <code>\unsure{\prime&#124;*}</code>; Thick raised mark does not preserve clear individual arms. (low).
- NB crossed fragment: <code>\deleted{\unsure{de}}</code>; Only d-like loop and short continuation survive. (low).
- NB verb ending: <code>\unsure{bornions&#124;bornons}</code>; Raised dot favors i but the compressed ending remains uncertain. (medium).
- Last sentence verb: <code>\unsure{pense&#124;peux}</code>; Shallow compressed terminal strokes admit both readings. (low).

### Remaining doubts

- Upper margin tail, first short word: <code>\unsure{en}</code>; The low initial word is only a short looped run before the secure tous ces. (low).
- Upper margin tail, word after tous ces: <code>\unsure{autres}</code>; The run has an apparent tall medial stroke and low ending; it cannot be confidently read as either witness's objets/cas. (low).
- Middle margin cancellation: <code>\deleted{on \unsure{peut}}</code>; The stroke across the word masks its medial letters. (low).
- Middle margin parenthetical start: <code>\unsure{N&#x27;est} \illegible{?}</code>; The intersecting body strokes obscure the segmentation after the N’est-like beginning. No independent pair of word spaces establishes exactly two unreadable words. Preserve the physically unreadable extent with an unknown count; do not supply a completion. (low).
- Lower margin after sais: <code>\unsure{i.e.}</code>; Tiny detached points and short strokes occur before the clear via; they are consistent with i.e. but insufficiently separated. (low).

- Word after et in the last line: <code>\unsure{la&#124;\deleted{la}}</code>; No separate accent is visible. The lower stroke is compatible with normal joining, but cancellation cannot be excluded. (medium).

### Remaining doubts

- Early cancelled opening: <code>\unsure{Rien qu&#x27;en}</code>; Crossing stroke merges with the small letter bodies. (low).
- Early cancelled unreadable run: <code>\illegible{?}</code>; An exact word count is unestablished; C’s 3 is not independently supported. (low).
- Margin opening: <code>\unsure{On l $=$.&#124;On lie}</code>; Literal l-like loop, two horizontal strokes and point are clearer than their linguistic interpretation. (low).
- Cancelled lifted-action sign: <code>\unsure{\widetilde{\Theta}&#124;\widetilde{\Theta}_0}</code>; Low-right trace is inseparable from cancellation. (low).
- Display52 link: <code>\overset{\sim}{\unsure{\longrightarrow&#124;\mathbin{-}}}</code>; Terminal looks blunt or hooked; no secure two-arm head. (low).
- Toute telle endings: <code>\unsure{toute telle}</code>; Both endings are retraced; grammar is not used to alter them. (medium).
- Abbreviation beneath bel: <code>\unsure{p.e.&#124;i.e.}</code>; Small first upright remains ambiguous. (low).
- Inserted E0 link: <code>\overset{\sim}{\unsure{\to&#124;\mathbin{-}}}</code>; Small thick endpoint does not settle arrowhood. (low).
- Crossed clause beneath inserted map: <code>\deleted{\unsure{qui soit}}</code>; Q-like descender and final t-like stroke survive overlapping additions. (low).
- Crossed word before live pour: <code>\deleted{\unsure{pour}}</code>; Horizontal strike hides most letter bodies. (low).
- Old ink beneath E0: <code>\deleted{\unsure{de}}</code>; Looped older ink is entangled with the raised replacement. (low).
- Final pourtant: shared t-crossbar or cancellation: <code>\unsure{pourtant&#124;\deleted{pourtant}}</code>; The word pourtant is readable. The long upper line runs between the two t stems at crossbar height, above the low a/n bodies. It can be a shared crossbar; an independent strike through pour or through the low letter bodies is not securely established. Prefer live pourtant, retaining the deleted alternative because the function of that long stroke is not fully settled. (medium).

### Remaining doubts

- isolated sign after contexte des groupoïdes,: <code>\unsure{$\{$}</code>; The sign is present but its precise identity and function are not established by its loops/stem. (low).
- subscript of g(u): <code>g_{\unsure{\ell&#124;e}}(u)</code>; The narrow tall loop favors ell, but an e-like construction cannot be excluded. (medium).
- opening sign before des paramètres: <code>\unsure{$\{$&#124;(}</code>; Middle loop and long lower stroke are unlike an ordinary smooth parenthesis, but do not exclude a retraced one. (low).
- cancelled word below tels que: <code>\deleted{\unsure{comme}}</code>; The cancellation obscures the interior and ending; surviving low strokes do not establish the whole word. (low).

### Remaining doubts

- Pronoun after Mais: <code>\unsure{il&#124;je}</code>; Overlapping upright and lower return strokes leave the current pronoun unsettled; no independent deleted pronoun is supplied. (low).
- Canceled word before semble: <code>\deleted{\unsure{paraît}}</code>; The p descender and ending favor paraît, but the cancellation covers the middle. (medium).
- Underlined marginal noun: <code>\uline{\unsure{opérations&#124;orientations}}</code>; The p/ri cluster is visually ambiguous; the following sur un ensemble provides contextual preference for opérations only. (low).
- Small parenthetical word in margin: <code>\unsure{sur}</code>; Tiny strokes after the opening parenthesis overlap body writing. (low).
- Margin preposition before groupoïde: <code>\unsure{du}</code>; Oblique body strokes intersect the small marginal forms. (low).
- Margin noun before fondamental: <code>\unsure{groupoïde}</code>; The start and overall run fit groupoïde, but body overlap prevents an unqualified reading. (low).
- Margin formula field index: <code>X_{\unsure{K}}</code>; The K-like outline is crossed by a separate body stroke. (medium).
- Final short margin row: <code>\unsure{purement&#124;seulement}\ldots</code>; The initial loop/descender and neighboring f descent overlap; the final three points are distinct. (low).
- Field index in inserted nonemptiness condition: <code>\unsure{X_{\overline{K}}&#124;X_K}</code>; The short mark over K may be live or canceled/retraced. The distinction affects the condition and remains explicit. (low).
- Opening paragraph, proper name after et des groupes de: <code>Teichm\unsure{û&#124;ü}ller</code>; Above the u immediately before the tall double-l, there is one joined short dark arch/hook with a raised middle, not two separated points. The joined shape favors a circumflex and resembles the arch over i in the separately visible apparaître; a joined umlaut gesture remains possible. The uncertainty is explicit rather than a normalization to the familiar spelling. (medium).
